Nebius 10/1 全⾯調漲  Nvidia AI GPU ， CPU ， Memory 租⽤價
格，我們認為短期市場價值從賣  tokens 的⼤語⾔ / 專家語⾔模型公司

根據路透社  （Nebius hikes AI cloud prices again as demand for
computing power soars）， Euronext ， MarketBeat 9 ⽉  17 ⽇報導，
Nebius Group 宣布⾃  2026 年  10 ⽉  1 ⽇起局部調升  On-demand 算⼒租
⾦：其中  Nvidia H100 租⽤價格由每⼩時  US$3.85 調升  16.9% ⾄
US$4.50 ；次世代旗艦  B300 算⼒租⾦更⼤漲  21% 從每⼩時 US$7.85 調升
到  US$9.50 。除此以外， Nebius 也調⾼部分 AMD/Intel x86 CPU 租⽤價格
達 25% ，及存儲記憶體租⽤價格達 41% ， Nebius 同步確認其簽約容量全速朝
向年底  5 GW ⽬標邁進。同⽇， CoreWeave 官⽅宣布⾃  6 ⽉底以來持續以
更⾼價格簽約算⼒，其第三季度簽訂的  3 ⾄  6 個⽉短期客戶合約，年化定價

達到每  MW 約  4,000 萬美元（折合  1 GW 算⼒租⽤合約每年⾼達  400 億美
元），合約總⽤電容量⾃  3.7 GW 增⾄  4.2 GW 。為⽀撐龐⼤建置，
CoreWeave 同步啟動  30 億美元可轉換優先票據發售（另附  5 億美元超額配
售權），並設⽴⾼達  3,500 萬股（約  29.2 億美元）的市價增發（ ATM ）融資
機制。

左右）的強⼤⽀撐來反應 AI 算⼒數據中⼼基礎建設，電⼒及晶⽚價格價格的飆

## Document 017: 20260915【投研報告】市場快訊_市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦Fed決策與財報

Source PDF: all report pdf/20260915【投研報告】市場快訊_市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦Fed決策與財報.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (市場評論)
Date: 2026-09-15

市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

不另行通知。
三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

科技動能支撐股市，利率風險牽動債市配置
2026 年 9 月
全球股市 8 月截至 26 日多數上漲，其中成熟市場上漲 3.1%居冠，新興亞洲、東協股市與標普
500 也分別上漲 2.9%、2.7%及 2.6%。美股受企業財報韌性與月初就業、通膨數據降溫支撐；亞洲
則受美元偏弱、油價與長端殖利率回落帶動風險偏好改善。然而，中國股市期間下跌 0.3%，該國 7
月零售、工業生產與投資數據偏弱，加上阿里巴巴折價配股一度衝擊科技股，惟公司高層後續增持有
助緩解信心壓力 。 拉美股市下跌0.8%，在主要股市中敬陪末座 ， 主要拖累來自巴西 ；8 月截至 24 日，

外資淨流出巴西股市約 214 億雷亞爾，加上財政與 10 月大選的不確定性壓抑市場。印度亦小跌
0.3%，因高油價與 RBI 偏鷹訊號使公債殖利率上升。
全球債市 8 月截至 26 日則全面收紅 ， 以新興當地債上漲1.53%居冠 ， 美元指數回落至99 附近，
為其帶來匯兌加成；亞洲高收益債與全球高收益債分別上漲 1.16%、1.09%，高票息與較短存續期提
供支撐 。 亞洲債券 、 全球主權債與新興美元債表現相對落後 。8 月中美國 30 年期殖利率一度升至 2007
年以來高點，壓抑長存續期債券；其後油價回落，加上8/19 財政部宣布 9/9 起將 10 至 30 年期公債
買回上限至少倍增至每次 40 億美元，長端流動性疑慮獲緩解，30 年期殖利率回落，因而帶動債券價
格修復。
展望 9 月，市場焦點將集中於 9 月 15 日至 16 日的 FOMC 會議，以及最新經濟預測與點陣圖；
全球股市預料呈現高檔震盪格局。儘管物價漲勢稍有緩和，但 Fed 對通膨仍保持警戒；若政策立場
較市場預期鷹派，可能推升美債殖利率波動，並壓抑高估值成長股走勢。不過，AI 基礎建設與企業

科技投資趨勢延續，美國企業獲利成長亦有利科技股表現，預料中期基本面仍具支撐。台灣受 AI 與
半導體出口需求帶動，經濟成長動能強勁；日股則持續受企業治理改革與資本效率改善支撐。整體而
言，9 月股市短線震盪難免，但中期仍看好科技股，以及基本面相對強勁的台灣與日本股市。
就債市而言，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔；若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利
率上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短的存續期間亦伴隨再投資
風險；10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。相較之下，3 至 10 年期債券兼具較高息收、
適度存續期間與參與利率下行的空間。整體而言，目前 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬
條件相對均衡，較具表現機會。

美國股市：AI 熱潮推升企業獲利，美股續強但留意利率風險
八月美股在高檔震盪中收高，科技股表現亮眼，除整體美股優異財報支撐指數表現外，AI 龍頭企
業如 NVIDIA 均給出強勁展望 ，AI 行情由晶片硬體向軟體與資安應用端擴散 。指數上揚主要由輝達、
微軟、亞馬遜、帕蘭提爾、特斯拉、Meta、美光科技、賽富時、Marvell、默克藥廠等企業帶動，表
現相對弱勢的族群包括蘋果、博通、沃爾瑪、TJX、AppLovin、威騰、聯合健康、CVS、應用材料、
超微半導體等。
聯準會新任主席試圖以新的溝通模式取代傳統前瞻指引，市場解讀為基準利率有上行空間且更長

時間維持高檔。聯準會主席華許在全球央行年會(Jackson Hole)拒絕提供傳統的前瞻指引或機械式的
反應函數，強調央行過度承諾未來利率路徑會失去政策彈性，以一套「決策準則」取代指引，重點觀
察潛在通膨是否快速回到 2%、勞動市場是否偏離充分就業，以及金融條件是否有效限制總需求。由
於 7 月 FOMC 會議出現官員主張升息的情況，使短端與長端美債殖利率走勢分化，殖利率曲線進一
步陡峭化 ， 市場正在質疑 Fed 是否具備一致且可理解的決策框架 ， 但在華許發言後 ， 按照這套標準，
目前政策訊號仍偏向利率在較高水準維持更長時間。由於通膨問題仍具廣度，同時信用利差偏低、股
市高檔、AI 投資與私人需求持續強勁，很難將目前金融條件視為具有限制性，故市場預期 聯準會可
能不只提高升息機率，也提高了升息後維持高利率的可能性，甚至進一步提高未來降息的門檻。
第二季財報公布大致落幕，表現優於先前預期。目前標普 500 成分股已有 459 家公布 2Q26 財
報，其中 86%獲利優於市場預期，並帶動 2Q26 獲利年增達約 31.6%，較季初市場預估的 21%大幅

上修；全年獲利年增亦由 23%上修至 27%。類股獲利年增動能以能源 (YoY+145%)、資訊科技
(YoY+71%)、原材料(YoY+41%)增長動能最強，醫療保健類股(YoY-7%)表現落後。資訊科技類股獲
利成長主要由半導體族群帶動，次產業中半導體獲利年增 139%、科技硬體與設備獲利年增 50.9%。
美國期中選舉前的「資料中心用電排擠效應引發的民粹反彈」持續發酵。隨著 AI 資料中心龐大
的電力需求，引發地方民意對民生與傳統產業用電資源遭擠壓的疑慮持續升溫 。 各州政府在選舉壓力
下，部分地方政府緊急凍結資料中心計畫，預料 11 月期中選舉後政治干擾將可降溫。
目前標普 500 指數位於歷史高檔位置，本益比已升至 23.51 倍、股價淨值比達 5.6 倍，短期評價
偏高。雖然美國科技業者目前仍居 AI 產業領先地位，但股市漲幅已高，短期仍需留意市場過熱與估
值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：景氣溫和復甦，歐洲央行升息預期與政治變數限制評價 
八月歐股延續多頭走勢，泛歐600 指數中原物料與科技股表現相對強勢，包括思愛普、艾司摩爾
控股、Argenx、施耐德電機、力拓、嘉能可、英美資源、安聯集團、勞斯萊斯控股、西門子等表現
較佳；相較之下，部分金融與消費股表現偏弱，包括雀巢、匯豐控股、諾和諾德、英美菸草、阿斯特
捷利康、法國巴黎銀行、法國興業銀行、諾華、百威英博、萬喜等。 
歐元區八月綜合 PMI 升至 52.1，連續第 2 個月站上榮枯線，顯示第 3 季初景氣延續擴張。其中
製造業升至 52.8，創 2022 年 5 月以來最佳表現，復甦已由生產端擴散至需求端。AI 相關科技與設
備需求增加，加上國防支出擴大，帶動製造業需求回升；服務業則持平於 51.7，受惠於旅遊維持溫
和擴張。國家別來看，德國製造業 PMI 升至 54.1，創 2022 年以來最佳表現，法國製造業重返擴張
區間，但極端高溫拖累服務業活動。整體而言，八月 PMI 顯示歐元區景氣動能明顯改善，製造業加
速復甦，短期衰退風險進一步下降，然而服務業擴張力道未見加快，復甦步調呈現分化。 
部分歐洲央行官員表示，九月已有充分理由升息，利率交換市場目前反映 ECB 在 9 月會議升息
一碼的可能性。美伊戰爭推高能源成本，加上歐洲經濟表現穩健，使市場認為 ECB 仍有收緊貨幣政
策的空間。市場焦點因此由「ECB 會不會升息」轉向「升息循環還會延續多久」 。中東局勢仍是最大
變數，若能源成本持續走高且逐步傳導至商品、服務價格與薪資，ECB 第四季仍可能維持鷹派立場。 
9 月歐股須留意政治局勢。若 AfD（德國另類選擇黨）於地方選舉取得大勝，雖不會直接改變目
前政府執政地位，但可能削弱改革推進速度，造成市場風險溢價上升；另外英國新任首相柏南承諾維
持既有財政規則，並受到工黨不提高所得稅、國民保險及主要稅率承諾的約束，使政府在國防、公共
服務與生活成本支出增加之際 ， 財政操作空間相當有限 ， 市場因此高度關注政府是否轉向其他形式的
增稅。若改革措施過度激進推高英國公債殖利率，恐將造成市場震盪。 
歐洲經濟整體呈現溫和復甦，但原油價格再次走高是最大隱憂。若能源成本居高不下，歐洲央行
與英國央行第四季仍可能維持鷹派立場 ， 進而導致產業表現分化 。 銀行與金融類股可望受惠於利率走
高及淨利差改善；反之，科技、醫療保健及其他成長型類股將面臨殖利率上升所帶來的評價壓力。目
前泛歐 600 指數本益比約 16.5 倍，股價淨值比約 2.5 倍。雖然俄烏戰爭與通膨再度升溫風險仍需關
注，但在評價合理的背景下，歐股仍適合作為長線分批布局標的。

資料來源：彭博,2026/8/31

日本股市：通膨回溫拉高升息預期，半導體與內需動能推升經濟表現
八月日股受地緣政治風險與殖利率走高影響，日經225 指數劇烈震盪。盤面上，強勢族群包括瑞
可利控股、愛德萬測試、藤倉、軟銀、松下控股、村田製作所、瑞穗金融、東京威力科創、三菱UFJ
金融、揖斐電等；弱勢股包括東京海上控股、迅銷、大金工業、富士軟片、日本電裝、發那科、7&I、
丸紅、損保控股、KDDI 等。
8 月東京核心 CPI 升至 1.8%，雖能源補貼與食品價格降溫壓抑整體物價，但租金與醫療服務價
格明顯上升；扣除食品與能源後通膨加速至 1.4%，顯示內生性通膨壓力仍在。若就十大產品分項來

看，引導本月通膨的上漲主要來自房屋及醫療保健，其中租金價格年增幅達 1.9%，反映物價上漲持
續傳遞至房市 ； 醫療保健價格年增幅亦自前月的1.4%攀升至 2.8%， 因政府將高額療養費制度內的自
付費用上限調高所致 。 根據總務省估算 ， 日本政府對能源補貼的政策約壓抑本月通膨0.27 個百分點，
顯示若扣除政策影響，則當前通膨約為 2.2%，仍高於日本央行所設定的物價目標。雖政府補貼政策
將持續對通膨帶來壓抑效果，使得整體通膨略低於 2%，然而搭配央行對日圓匯率過於弱勢、恐進一
步墊高通膨的擔憂，預估將促使日本央行最快於 9 月份升息一碼。
日本半導體製造裝置協會 （SEAJ） 公布7 月日本製晶片設備銷售額 （3 個月移動平均值） 為5,562.2
億日圓，月銷售額年增 35.4%，創下 2024 年 12 月以來最大增幅。同時，若觀察今年 1 至 7 月份累
計銷售額達 3.44 兆日圓，年增 15.7%，顯示隨著當前人工智慧墊高晶片需求，推升廠商對半導體設
備的需求。
日本 7 月外國旅客入境人數為 344.2 萬人次，年增 0.1%。本月份外國旅客人數最大宗仍是來自

南韓，達 89.5 萬人，年增 31.9%。其次則為台灣遊客的 76.4 萬人次，年增 26.4%。與之相反，來
自中國的遊客人數年減幅達 56.1%， 因日中兩國關係遲遲未見改善所致 。 根據日本百貨店協會公布的
資料顯示，7 月百貨公司免稅銷售額年增 25.5%至 506 億日圓，連續五個月的正成長，顯示在日圓
延續弱勢的格局之下，外國旅客消費力仍有提升空間。
2026 年來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、日圓貶值、高市擴大財政刺激與強化軍事安全等政
策利多支撐，雖然日圓在政府干預與 BOJ 升息下繼續貶值空間受限，出口企業的匯兌紅利將逐步消
退，但預料日圓接下來仍以區間整理機會較高，並無大幅升值空間。目前 TOPIX 指數本益比約 17.7
倍、股價淨值比約 1.9 倍，評價仍屬合理，可利用市場震盪機會逢低分批布局。

台灣股市：工業生產創高、景氣續亮紅燈，AI 供應鏈撐起製造業擴張動能
8 月台股在 AI 龍頭財報優於預期及全球 AI 資本支出持續擴張帶動下，市場氣氛維持偏多，但受
到 Jackson Hole 會議後市場重新調整聯準會政策預期、美債殖利率攀升及美元轉強影響，指數於高
檔呈現震盪整理。資金面上，高評價 AI 族群漲多後出現輪動，市場開始重新檢視企業未來獲利成長
能否持續支撐評價水準。不過整體而言，AI 資料中心、半導體、先進封裝及伺服器供應鏈需求仍維
持強勁，有助於消化部分評價壓力，並使市場焦點重新回到企業基本面與獲利成長。

當前臺灣經濟仍處於景氣擴張階段。全球 AI 建置需求推動企業設備投資與科技資本支出維持高
檔，相關需求正逐步由晶片擴散至資料中心、網通設備、電力基礎建設及相關零組件。從國際環境觀
察，美國企業投資及私人需求維持韌性，AI 相關支出已成為支撐美國與全球科技景氣的重要力量，
也持續為台灣出口與半導體供應鏈提供結構性成長動能。
展望 9 月，市場關注焦點將由企業財報逐漸轉向總體經濟數據與聯準會政策方向。Jackson Hole
會議後，市場對於聯準會維持較高利率期間的預期升溫，短期可能提高全球金融市場波動。然而，從
企業基本面觀察，NVIDIA 最新展望顯示 AI 需求仍具高度能見度，全球雲端業者與大型企業的 AI 基
礎建設支出並未出現明顯放緩跡象。在此背景下，只要半導體、伺服器及先進封裝產業維持強勁接單
動能，台灣電子產業景氣仍有機會延續高檔表現。
投資策略方面，仍可維持對 AI 供應鏈的正向看法，但須更重視獲利成長與評價合理性。除了先
進製程及先進封裝外，AI 伺服器組裝、散熱、電源管理、高速傳輸、網通設備及記憶體等受惠 AI 基

建擴張之族群，仍具備中長期成長機會。隨著AI 投資逐步由 GPU 擴散至記憶體、網路交換器、光通
訊及系統整合等領域 ， 受惠範圍亦由少數龍頭供應商進一步擴展至整體供應鏈 ， 有助於支撐相關企業
未來營收與獲利成長動能。另一方面，9 月需留意美國 CPI 數據、聯準會利率決策及美債殖利率變化
對高評價科技股的影響；若市場因政策預期、資金輪動或短期獲利了結而出現回檔，較可能屬於多頭
趨勢中的震盪整理，而非基本面反轉。在企業獲利持續成長、AI 資本支出維持高檔及全球科技景氣
延續復甦的背景下 ， 建議布局方向可由單純題材面轉向具備技術門檻 、 訂單能見度及現金流品質較佳
的個股，以提升投資組合在景氣循環與市場波動中的穩健度。

中國股市：硬科技 IPO 熱潮延燒，A 股中報揭露「增收不增利」普遍現象
8 月中國經濟延續「總量承壓、科技產業相對強勢」的分化格局。儘管房地產調整與內需復甦力
道仍有限，但高端製造、人工智慧及數位經濟仍是政策資源與市場資金聚焦方向。在傳統產業景氣復
甦較為緩慢的情況下 ， 科技產業逐漸成為支撐經濟成長的重要動能 ， 顯示中國經濟正持續朝新舊動能
轉換與產業升級方向發展。
科技產業方面，人形機器人已成為近期最受市場關注的新興產業之一。根據中國國家市場監管總
局統計，2026 年上半年人形機器人領域新設企業數量達 11.6 萬家，較 2025 年同期的 7.2 萬家大幅

成長 61.1%，反映資本投入、技術研發與產業參與程度快速提升。隨著人工智慧、大模型及感測技術
進步，人形機器人正逐步由研發驗證邁向商業 化應用階段，產業發展重點也由概念題材轉向量產能
力、產品可靠度及終端需求驗證。
資本市場表現雖顯示科技板塊仍是 A 股資金主要聚焦方向 ， 但企業基本面改善幅度與市場期待之
間仍存在落差。從滬深 300 成分股來看，科技類企業近年營收維持增長，但獲利表現並未同步大幅
提升，反映部分企業仍面臨研發投入增加、產能擴張及市場競爭加劇等壓力。AI、半導體及高端製造
雖持續獲得政策支持與資本關注 ， 但不少企業的估值已提前反映未來成長預期 ， 實際獲利貢獻仍有待
進一步驗證。市場關注焦點也逐漸由政策題材與自主替代概念，轉向訂單能見度、現金流與獲利能力
等基本面指標；若後續營運表現未能跟上市場預期，不排除科技板塊仍面臨評價修正壓力。
展望 9 月，政策方向預計仍以穩增長與培育新質生產力為主軸，人工智慧、人形機器人、高端製
造及半導體等領域仍將是支持重點 。 在整體經濟復甦力道尚未全面擴散的背景下 ， 市場表現預期仍將

呈現結構性分化，資金持續聚焦具成長潛力與技術門檻的科技產業。投資策略上，可留意人形機器人
產業鏈、工業自動化、AI 應用、半導體設備及高端製造等受惠產業升級趨勢之族群；惟部分熱門題
材評價已反映較高成長預期，後續仍須觀察訂單落地、量產進度及獲利改善情況，避免因短期市場情
緒過熱而提高投資風險。

科技類股：AI 基礎建設持續擴張，算力、光通訊與平台生態同步受惠
全球科技產業的核心成長動能仍來自 AI 基礎建設擴張 ， 但市場焦點已逐步由單純GPU 採購轉向
整體資料中心架構升級 。 隨Google 擴大投入光通訊與高速網路技術 ，AI 資料中心正朝向更高頻寬、
更低延遲及更高能效方向發展 。 市場預估800G 至 1.6T 光通訊滲透率將持續提升 ， 推動光模組 、CPO、
交換器及相關供應鏈需求成長，也反映大型雲端服務供應商仍持續強化 AI 基礎建設布局。在此趨勢
下，AI 投資已由單一晶片擴展至網路、儲存、記憶體及先進封裝等完整生態系，帶動相關供應鏈重

新評價。
企業獲利方面，NVIDIA 最新財報再次驗證 AI 投資循環仍具延續性。公司第二季營收與資料中心
業務均大幅優於預期，資料中心網路業務亦維持高速成長，顯示大型企業、雲端服務業者及 AI 模型
開發商對算力需求仍然強勁。同時，管理層對第三季營收及新一代 Vera Rubin 平台維持正向展望，
反映 AI 基礎建設建置並未出現明顯放緩跡象。不過，市場對AI 產業的評價標準已逐漸由需求成長轉
向獲利品質與投資效益，未來資本支出、折舊攤提與現金流變化仍將是投資人關注重點。
從產業鏈角度來看，AI 需求正持續向外擴散。除了 GPU 之外，高速光通訊、網路交換器、高頻
寬記憶體(HBM)、CPU 及資料中心電力架構均成為下一階段重要受惠領域。另一方面，NVIDIA 近期
宣布結合金融機構與雲端服務資源打造大型 AI 基礎設施平台，顯示產業發展正由硬體銷售進一步延
伸至算力服務與平台營運模式。此舉有利強化 AI 產業生態系並提高資本取得效率，但也反映未來競
爭重點將由單純硬體性能逐步轉向平台整合能力、商業模式及資金運用效率。隨著更多企業投入 AI

建設，市場也開始關注高額資本支出是否能持續帶來相應回報，產業獲利表現仍可能出現明顯分化。
展望後續，AI 仍是科技產業最具確定性的投資主軸，但市場關注焦點正由 「建置多少算力」逐步
轉向「創造多少效益」。NVIDIA 新平台推廣進度、Vera Rubin 產品放量、高速光通訊滲透率提升
及 HBM 供給變化，將是觀察下一階段 AI 投資循環的重要指標。雖然大型雲端業者資本支出尚未出
現明顯下修，支撐整體供應鏈需求維持高檔，但隨著資料中心建置成本、能源支出與折舊費用持續增
加 ， 企業獲利能力與現金流表現的重要性亦將提升 。 未來較具競爭優勢的企業 ， 除了掌握核心技術外 ，
更需具備規模化商業模式及資本效率，才能在 AI 產業由高速成長邁向成熟發展的過程中持續創造價
值。

礦業類股：關稅預期有利銅價支撐，戰略庫存需求是潛在利多
8 月以來礦業類股走勢強勁，倫敦金屬交易所銅價也再創歷史新高，這波上漲的主要動能，仍是
來自於我們先前一再提到的美國潛在銅關稅，改變了全球銅庫存的流向 。 由於市場擔心美國未來可能
對精煉銅加徵關稅，交易商提前將銅運往美國，希望在關稅實施前完成進口，導致其他地區能夠取得
的現貨供應相應減少，在供給減少下持續為銅價帶來支撐。
截至 8 月底，美國現行關稅主要涵蓋銅棒、銅線、銅板及銅管等半成品與衍生產品，精煉銅尚未
正式納入課稅範圍，但市場普遍預估美國政府將有可能在 2027 年開始實施精煉銅關稅，帶動交易商

提前採取備貨行動。路透社報導指出，美國2026 年上半年進口約 88.5 萬噸精煉銅，年增約 3%，紐
約商品交易所銅庫存也攀升至約 67.5 萬噸的歷史高點。若將時間拉長至一年觀察，全球三大交易所
合計增加約 49.58 萬噸庫存，其中約 81%集中於紐約商品交易所，顯示全球可見銅庫存雖然上升，
但新增庫存大多被鎖在美國，非美市場銅庫存相對處於較為緊張的狀態。
除了關稅帶動的庫存 失衡外，銅的實際應用需求也具有基本面支撐。彭博行業研究 預估，2026
年全球銅需求將成長 2.4%至 2.7%，增速雖較 2025 年放緩，但電動車、再生能源、資料中心及電網
升級仍將帶動用銅需求上升。其中雖然中國銅需求成長預估也將放緩，但是中國國家電網已規劃 2026
至 2030 年投資 4 兆元人民幣，較前五年增加 44%，以支應人工智慧用電需求。
另一方面，隨著區域銅庫存出現短缺及 AI 帶動用銅需求上升，若政府與企業在未來計畫提高安
全庫存，將可能成為市場尚未充分反映的中長期上漲基礎。花旗研究估算，目前全球精煉銅總庫存約

為 300 萬噸，僅相當於 1.3 個月的全球消費量。若各國政府及大型供應鏈基於國防、能源安全、人
工智慧基礎建設或地緣政治風險 ， 將安全庫存提高至兩個月 ， 市場約需要額外累積 170 萬噸銅庫存；
若提高至三個月，額外需求更將擴大至約 400 萬噸。由於新礦山從開發到投產需要多年，供給無法
在短期內迅速增加，市場只能透過銅價上漲，促使更多廢銅進入市場，同時迫使部分使用者節約用銅
或尋找替代材料。因此，建立庫存的規模愈大、完成時間愈短，銅價需要調整的漲幅也將愈顯著。
展望後市，儘管美國精煉銅關稅目前尚未正式實施，但市場預期帶來的提前備貨潮仍有利短線銅
價支撐，同時全球用銅需求仍維持成長，新礦山供給又難以快速實現，加上政府及企業可能逐步提高
安全庫存 ， 使銅市場的中長期供需結構仍具有支撐 ， 故對具銅價曝險的礦業類股中長期後市仍可正向
看待 ， 然而仍需留意若未來銅關稅未如市場預期實施 ， 當前囤積於美國市場的銅庫存將可能回歸到全
球市場當中，為銅價帶來短線波動。

能源類股：荷莫茲海峽持續受阻，惟實際供應缺口與庫存降幅低於市場預期
8 月以來，美伊衝突持續反覆升降溫，雙方時而釋出談判訊號，時而再度發生軍事衝突，短期內
仍未出現實質進展，荷莫茲海峽通行亦持續受限。然而，海峽受阻並不代表原有運量全數消失，部分
原油仍可經由海運與陸上替代管線出口 ，同時中國石油需求下降及各國政府釋放戰略庫存亦抵銷部分
中斷影響。
彭博行業研究指出，儘管當前荷莫茲海峽受到干擾，但實際並未呈現完全封鎖的狀態，目前仍有
零星油輪持續通行。彭博指出，由於部分油輪會關閉公開定位訊號，並在通過海峽後於阿曼外海進行

轉運，因此公開船舶資料可能會漏算部分運量。彭博綜合目前庫存變化與市場實際供需後，估計目前
每日仍有約 750 萬桶原油通過荷莫茲海峽，意味僅依據可觀察船舶數量推算中東石油供應中斷量，
可能高估了市場實際缺口。
除了每日仍有約 750 萬桶原油經過海峽運輸外，彭博估計目前另有每日約 500 萬桶石油改走陸
上管線；需求端則因中國每日減少約 400 萬桶進口而下降，其他地區減少用油與各國政府釋放戰略
庫存再合計填補每日約 200 萬桶石油缺口。經過上述調整後，原本每日約 2,000 萬桶經荷莫茲海峽
通行的運量中，實際供應缺口僅剩每日約 150 萬桶，這也解釋了當前油價漲幅為何未完全反映表面
的產量損失。
全球石油庫存變化的數據亦呼應了實際缺口低於市場普遍預期的論點。 花旗報告指出 ，2026 年 2
月至 8 月全球實際觀察到的石油供需缺口平均約為每日 290 萬桶，明顯低於其原先預估的每日 700
萬至 800 萬桶。截至 2026 年 8 月，全球石油庫存仍可覆蓋 99 天需求，亦仍處於 2022 年至 2025

年的 95 至 101 天區間。花旗估計，若目前庫存下降速度延續，全球庫存要到 2029 年第一季才會降
至接近第二次石油危機緊張程度的 70 天水位，這顯示荷莫茲海峽若持續受阻，雖油價上漲壓力仍會
逐步累積，但實際庫存下降速度並未如市場普遍預期的嚴峻。
展望後市， 除荷莫茲海峽中斷造成的 供應缺口與庫存降幅低於市場預期 外，國際能源署 也預估
2027 年全球石油需求將增加每日 240 萬桶 ， 供應則可能年增每日830 萬桶 ， 意味若中東產量與海峽
通行逐步恢復，供應回升速度將明顯快於需求，並帶動庫存回補，不利油價上漲趨勢，然而考量短線
地緣風險仍有利能源類股形成支撐，在美伊達成長期停火協議前，應中性看待能源類股後市表現。

黃金類股：央行買盤支撐中長期價格，惟升息預期帶動短線波動升高
8 月金價走勢先揚後抑。月初公布的美國就業數據偏弱，加上中國及全球黃金 ETF 買盤回升，使
市場一度降低近期升息預期，金價因而突破 7 月的整理區間。到了 8 月中旬，美國財政部宣布擴大
長天期公債回購，帶動長端殖利率與美元回落，進一步放大黃金漲勢；不過，月底公布的通膨數據仍
顯示物價壓力具有黏性 ， 聯準會主席華許在傑克森霍爾會議的態度也偏向鷹派 ， 使市場重新提高升息
預期，金價隨即自高檔回落。然而即使月底出現修正，金價在 8 月仍累積明顯漲幅。
支撐金價中長期走勢的核心力量，仍然來自世界各國央行為分散外匯儲備，持續增持黃金。世界

黃金協會指出，2026 年第 2 季全球央行淨購金量為 289 噸，累計 2026 年上半年約 345 噸。雖然上
半年央行購金量受到土耳其、俄羅斯等國調節部位影響，降至2022 年以來同期低點，但第 2 季購金
量已較第 1 季明顯回升，而且買盤仍分布於波蘭、中國、烏茲別克及哈薩克等多個市場，顯示各國央
行增持黃金的趨勢仍未出現轉變，且可觀察到各國央行對於逢低承接黃金的意願。
在央行需求之外，中國投資資金是本輪金價突破的重要邊際買盤。2026 年上半年中國黃金淨進
口量達 764 噸，較 2025 年同期增加 138%，其規模明顯高於中國人民銀行公布的增持量，顯示民間
與機構投資人也在提高黃金配置 。 當中國資金推動金價突破整理區間後 ， 美國商品期貨交易委員會的
資料也顯示 ， 管理基金黃金淨多單在 8 月持續增加 ， 使原本集中於亞洲的漲勢進一步獲得美國期貨資
金支持。
然而，投資資金參與程度提高，也使金價對利率與美元變化更加敏感。美國財政部 8 月 19 日宣
布，將至少加倍 10 年至 30 年期公債的流動性支持回購規模，市場隨即下調長端殖利率，黃金也在

持有成本下降後快速上漲。不過，月底公布的美國 7 月通膨數據仍高，加上華許在傑克森霍爾會議維
持偏鷹立場，再次使市場重新認為聯準會仍可能透過升息抑制通膨，美元與殖利率回升後，部分短線
資金便出現獲利了結賣壓。
展望後市，各國央行增持黃金儲備仍支撐黃金的中長期多頭結構，而美國財政與債務可持續性疑
慮也將持續提高黃金的分散風險價值。不過，8 月底的回檔顯示，當金價漲勢由 ETF 及期貨資金放大
後，實質利率與美元只要轉為上升，短線市場部位便可能迅速調整，故在美國後續利率政策仍具有不
確定性的狀況下，對於黃金類股後市應中性看待。

固定收益：長端風險仍存，3~10 年期公債、投資級債更具表現機會
8 月以來，美國公債殖利率維持高檔震盪，若以 7 月底與 8/24 比較，各年期債券殖利率近乎平
行小幅下行。8 月初公布的 7 月非農就業意外減少 2.3 萬人，顯示勞動市場動能轉弱；7 月 CPI 年增
率由 3.5%降至 3.4%，核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.6%降至 2.5%，一度緩解市場對 Fed 緊縮疑慮。惟 7
月 PPI 與 PCE 數據表現，限制利率進一步下行空間。
8 月中旬之後，市場焦點由政策利率逐步轉向能源價格、財政供給與期限溢酬。美伊局勢反覆推

升油價，加上美國聯邦債務8 月首度突破 40 兆美元，2 年與 30 年期利差由 7 月底 99 個基點擴大至
8/18 的 109 個基點，30 年期殖利率亦升至 5.28%。美國財政部 8/19 宣布，自 9/9 起將部分 10～
30 年期公債單次流動性回購上限由 20 億美元提高至至少 40 億美元，帶動 30 年期殖利率由 5.28%
快速回落至 5.19%。截至 8/24，2 年與 30 年利差亦回到 99 個基點，月中陡峭化大致回吐。此一走
勢顯示 ，回購措施雖有助改善長券流動性 ， 但財政赤字 、公債供給及期限溢酬等結構性壓力仍未消失 。
全球主要成熟國家的長端債券亦面臨類似壓力。德國 30 年期公債殖利率近期升至約 3.79%，創
2011 年以來最高；日本 10 年期公債殖利率 8/18 盤中一度觸及 2.945%，亦為 1996 年 9 月以來高
位，30 年期亦一度升至 4.115%。成熟市場長債同步承壓，反映財政擴張、通膨風險與貨幣政策正常
化正共同推升長端期限溢酬。

不過，信用市場整體仍相對穩定，截至 8/24，聖路易聯準銀行的 ICE 美國銀行美國投資級公司
債 OAS 約 81 個基點、有效殖利率約 5.4%，但信用利差仍處歷史偏低區間，緩衝並不寬裕。AI 基礎
建設融資的供給壓力亦已開始反映在定價，Reuters 8/21 指出，2026 年至 8 月上旬主要 AI 雲端業
者發債規模已約 2,200 億美元，科技類投資級債利差升至 89 個基點，較整體 IG 市場高出約 9 個基
點；近期 Alphabet 新券亦需提供約 10～15 個基點讓利，顯示在企業基本面仍穩健之際，大量新券
供給已成為影響信用評價的重要因素。
展望 9 月，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔，若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利率
上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短存續期間亦有再投資風險；
10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。值得注意的是，截至 8/27，10 年與 30 年期殖利率
利差為 52 個基點，2 年至 30 年總利差 96 個基點，顯示 2 年至 30 年期殖利率曲線陡峭幅度中，逾

半來自 10~30 年區間，反映超長端相對 10 年期仍有明顯期限溢酬。相較之下，3～10 年期兼具較
高息收、適度存續期間與利率下行參與空間。整體而言，目前 3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風
險報酬條件相對均衡，料具表現機會。

## Document 168: 8月宏观数据点评：结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月宏观数据点评：结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现.pdf
Report source: 银泰证券
Date: 2026-09-22
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现
——8 月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 9 月 15 日，国家统计局发布 8 月份国内宏观经济数据。8 月
份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.2%，社会消费品零售总额同比增长
0.4%；1—8 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 7.2%，房地产开发
投资同比下降 19.9%。
核心观点：
 数据上看，8 月国内经济延续分化格局。出口高增以及新兴产业快速发展
背景下，工业生产好于预期，新动能领域对全部规模以上工业增加值增
长贡献进一步增强；但与此同时受国内需求持续偏弱，稳增长政策尚未
全面发力等因素影响，社零、固定资产投资增速进一步走低。房地产市
场整体延续筑底格局，但二手房交易网签面积连续多个月高于新房销售
面积，同时一线城市房价企稳趋势继续得到确认，房地产市场结构性亮
点增多。
 8 月国内经济整体增长动能依然偏弱，后期经济表现在很大程度上仍将取
决于政策落地节奏。7 月底政治局会议定调“加快财政支出和债券资金使

用进度”以来，政策面积极信号增多，国家发改委多次召开项目协调会
推进“六张网”建设，中央预算内投资基本下达完毕，地方政府专项债
券的发行使用进度加快，9 月新型政策性金融工具开始全面投放。后期
伴随政策效果显现，国内经济增长动能有望边际改善。
 风险提示：1、政策力度不及预期；2、房地产调整超预期。
2026 年 9 月 21 日

1、出口与新动能支撑，工业生产好于预期
8 月份全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.2%，
较上月的 4.5%加快 0.7 个百分点，市场一致预期为
增长 4.7%。1—8 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长
5.3%，与 1—7 月持平。
8 月份工业生产表现好于预期，主要得益于外需
拉动与新兴产业发展的双重支撑。一方面，出口持续
高增为工业生产提供有力支撑。以美元计 8 月份我国
出口同比增长 25%，增速较上月进一步加快；受此带
动，当月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长
11.1%，增速较上月提升 0.7 个百分点，外需对工业
生产的拉动作用持续显现。另一方面，新兴产业快速
发展同样为工业生产注入强劲动能。8 月份，高技术
制造业增加值同比增长 16.7%，装备制造业增加值同
比增长 12.1%，分别高于全部规模以上工业增速 11.5
和 6.9 个百分点，对整体工业生产的支撑作用尤为突
出。除此之外，上年同期基数走低以及极端天气扰动

减弱同样带来一定影响。
分三大门类看，8 月份，采矿业增加值同比下降
1.4%（上月为-4.2%），制造业增长 6.1%（上月为
+5.5%），电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长

2、社零消费继续呈现偏弱格局
8 月份，全国社会消费品零售总额同比增长
0.4%，增速较上月的 0.6%回落 0.2 个百分点，市场

车类带来的拖累依然明显，扣除汽车类商品后的消费
品零售额增长 2.7%，增速与 1—7 月份持平。

增速较上月均有不同程度的放缓。8 月限额以上单位
消费品零售额同比下降 3.7%，自 4 月份以来已连续 5
个月负增长。

整体社零的拖累仍然较大；此外，家用电器和音像器
材类增长 2.3%（上月为-1.9%），增速由负转正；石
油及制品类下降 7.0%（上月为-7.6%）。
服务消费方面，1—8 月服务零售额累计同比增
长 4.9%（上月为+5.0%），增速整体保持稳定，继续
明显快于商品零售额增速（+1.0%）。在服务消费的
支撑下，1—8 月社会消费商品和服务零售总额同比
增长 2.5%，与 1—7 月的 2.6%基本持平。

3、固定资产增速降幅进一步扩大

（1—7 月为-3.6%），降幅继续扩大；制造业投资下
降 2.3%（1—7 月为-1.7%），今年 5 月份转负以来连
续 4 个月在负值区间运行。

化。1—8 月，高技术产业投资同比增长 5.2%，其中
信息服务业投资增长 22.7%，航空、航天器及设备制

Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei 在联合国安理会警告，若管理不善， AI 可能对全⼈类构成⽣

摘要：
Anthropic ⾸席执⾏官 Dario Amodei 在联合国安理会警告，若管理不善，⼈⼯智能可能对全⼈类
构成⽣存⻛险，并呼吁各国政府携⼿制定全球层⾯的 AI 安全标准。
Amodei 周三通过视频⽅式出席安理会会议，明确指出AI 存在被不法分⼦⽤于制造⽣物武器的⻛
险，同时也可能演变为开发者⽆法驾驭的失控系统。
Amodei 表⽰，出于安全考量， Anthropic 将放缓 AI 研发进度。
他敦促各国政府推进国际安全标准建设，并⽀持全球层⾯阻⽌ AI 被⽤于⽣化武器的相关努⼒。
此次表态是 Amodei 继本⽉初发表⼀篇约 3800 字的深度⽂章之后，再度公开呼吁对最先进 AI 系统

的研发加以约束，以使研究⼈员能够更好地评估潜在威胁。
他的发⾔对市场具有直接影响： Anthropic 正筹备 IPO ，估值或接近万亿美元，其明确的安全导
向⽴场将影响外界对该公司商业节奏与监管⻛险的预期。
AI 兼具重⼤机遇与灾难性⻛险
Amodei 在发⾔中对 AI 的前景作出双⾯评估。
他指出， AI 的快速发展正在推动健康科学领域的实质性进步，Anthropic 旗下 AI 模型 Claude 已
在⽣命科学研究中取得具体成果，包括发现⼀种带有类 CRISPR DNA 重复序列的新型酶系统。
然⽽，他同时强调，这⼀技术也携带潜在的灾难性后果。
在安全威胁⽅⾯，他特别点名⽣物武器⻛险。恐怖分⼦或不法⾏为者可能借助 AI 能⼒合成致命病
原体，这也是 Anthropic 专⻔成⽴⽣命科学研究实验室的背景之⼀。 Amodei 说：
呼吁超越竞争分歧，推进国际合作
Amodei 将此次安理会会议定位为各国超越分歧、寻求共识的关键时机。
他说：
这对全⼈类来说，既是机遇，也是挑战。
⽆论这个房间⾥的⼈存在多少分歧，我们都必须放下这些分歧，共同应对。没有任何⼀位领导

⼈、任何⼀家公司、任何⼀个国家能够独⾃应对这⼀挑战。

## Document 206: Asset Tokenization 101_ The Next Phase of Financial Infrastructure (2026-09-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Asset Tokenization 101_ The Next Phase of Financial Infrastructure (2026-09-16).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-16
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank Research Institute

### Page 2

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: Tokenization expands into market infrastructure
Market share of tokenized assets, Sep 14 2026
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Sep 14, 2026. “Distributed” denotes tokens
transferable outside the issuing platform and between wallets; it does not measure trading activity. Note:
Credit combines asset-backed credit, corporate credit, diversified credit and specialty finance. Other

assets comprise non-US government debt, private equity, venture capital and real estate.
– The global tokenized assets market, excluding stablecoins, grew from
~$10bn in Jan 2025 to ~$39bn in Sep 2026. US Treasury debt remains the
largest segment outside stablecoins, with money market funds leading
near-term adoption. Despite the US Digital Asset Market Clarity Act failing
to advance through to the Senate yesterday, we remain positive about the
momentum to tokenize both assets and capital markets in the coming

years.
– Institutions are increasingly applying tokenization to settlement, funding
and collateral management. Notable examples include DTCC’s tokenization
service, DL T-based intraday repo and collateral initiatives, and the
Eurosystem’s Pontes solution, which connects DL T platforms to TARGET
Services and is expected to launch later this month.
– A broad tokenized capital market requires progress across three areas:
assets, settlement money and market infrastructure. The tokenized RWA

market remains small relative to traditional markets, and token issuance
does not automatically create secondary-market liquidity.
– In this chartbook, we broaden our focus from tokenized assets, such as
bonds and stocks, to the infrastructure through which they are issued; and
review (i) market size; (ii) adoption trends; (iii) the regulatory drivers of
tokenization; and (iv) barriers that will determine the pace of growth.

### Page 4

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
– Tokenization records assets or claims as digital tokens on a
distributed ledger, using public or permissioned networks.
– Potential benefits include faster transfers, automated servicing and
smaller investment units.
– Shared records can reduce reconciliation, but trading hours,
redemption terms and investor access remain product-specific.
Two issuance models:
– On-chain (or native): The security or fund share is issued directly on a

distributed ledger.
– Off-chain (or tokenized representation): The underlying asset
remains outside the blockchain, but ownership claims and economic
rights are represented digitally on-chain.

### Page 5

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
STABLECOIN SPOTLIGHT: The settlement side of tokenization
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s FX Special Report: Tokenised money and the future of FX -
Deutsche Bank Research
– Stablecoins account for ~89% of the combined value of stablecoin and
distributed tokenized assets. We assess them separately because their
principal role is payment and settlement.
– They support near-instant, round-the-clock transfers for remittances,

business payments and treasury operations. Cross-border savings depend on
transaction fees, FX costs, compliance and conversion into bank money.
– Chainalysis projects adjusted annual transfer volume could rise from $28tn in
2025 to $719tn by 2035; higher scenario approaching $1.5 quadrillion.
Regulation and competition
– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
The GENIUS Act takes effect on the earlier of 18 January, 2027, or 120 days

after final implementing regulations are issued.
– Stablecoins could increase competition for bank deposits and payments.
Exchange rewards are distinct from issuer-paid interest, which GENIUS
prohibits for permitted payment stablecoins.
Risks
– Trust depends on reserve quality, legal redemption rights and operational
resilience. Stablecoins remain dependent on their issuers and supporting
institutions.
Tokenized deposits and central bank money provide alternative settlement options. Connecting the cash and securities legs of a transaction can improve settlement

efficiency and reduce fragmentation.
For more on tokenized money and settlement, particularly for FX, read here.

interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?

market cap here I would say.

### Page 6

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
– Early efforts focused on tokenized claims on traditional securities ( i.e. BlackRock’s
BUIDL, Franklin Templeton FOBXX), while the underlying assets remained off -chain.
– Financial institutions are now extending tokenization to the infrastructure through
which assets are distributed, traded, settled and financed.
DLT adoption: small, but intraday repo offers a practical use case

– Global DLT fixed-income issuance reached €4.8bn in 2025, up 48% from 2024.
– DLT intraday repo (i.e. JPM Kinexys and Broadridge’s DLR) can accelerate collateral
transfers and settlement, helping institutions manage short-term funding needs.
– A Finadium study estimated that using intraday DLR for 15% of activity could reduce
intraday liquidity buffer needs by 8–17%.
Tokenized securities on blockchain rails
– DTCC to launch its tokenization service in Oct 2026, covering Russell 1000 constituents,

ETFs tracking major indices, and US Treasury bills, notes and bonds.
– Initial production trades took place in July with >30 participating firms. Tokens are
recorded on DTCC's Hyperledger Besu network or on Canton, settling on either. The
SEC granted a three-year authorization.
Official-sector initiatives and interoperability
– Project Agorá, led by the BIS and IIF with central banks and financial institutions,
demonstrated a prototype for cross-border settlement using tokenized deposits and

central bank reserves. Real-value testing is the next phase.
– Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a joint interoperability framework with
BCG in March 2026 to address fragmentation across digital asset markets.
– Eurosystem’s Pontes solution connects DLT platforms to TARGET Services for
settlement in central bank money.

collateral

SHORT-TERM ADOPTION: Tokenized Treasuries and money market funds lead
Tokenized US Treasury market value reaches $15bn– Tokenized cash-management funds, including BUIDL and USYC,
combine short-term yields with more flexible transfers and collateral

use.
– Potential benefits include faster settlement and lower transaction costs,
making Treasury exposure more accessible across eligible digital
platforms.
– Direct Treasury bond tokenization accounted for 27% of overall US
Treasury tokenization in 2025, indicating adoption beyond fund
structures.
– These assets can also serve as collateral in repo and margin
arrangement, supporting demand beyond investment returns.
Secondary-market constraints
– Tokenized bonds are often held to maturity. Limited investor

participation and weak interoperability between platforms constrain
secondary-market liquidity.
– Some exchanges are attempting to improve liquidity by connecting
digital and conventional markets through dual listings, including SIX
digital exchange and Swiss Stock exchange).
Risks
– Redemption terms can differ from the settlement hours and liquidity of
underlying assets, creating potential liquidity mismatches.
– Blockchain transparency can improve visibility but may also accelerate

withdrawals during stress.
– Using tokenized fund shares across multiple products and as collateral
can create additional channels of contagion.

MEDIUM-TERM ADOPTION: Tokenized equities remain small; DTCC’s launch could
broaden adoption
Market value of tokenized equities
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data up to Sep 10, 2026.
– Tokenized equities remain small (<1%) relative to traditional stock markets.

– 68% of equity tokenization in 2025 consisted of direct tokenization of
individual stocks or baskets of stocks, vs. tokenized equity funds.
– Suggests that blockchain adoption is increasingly focused on the securities
themselves (i.e. DTCC method) rather than through third-party platforms (i.e.
Ondo and xStocks).
Developments
– DTCC’s tokenization service, scheduled to launch in Oct, will allow eligible
existing securities to move across blockchain infrastructure within its

regulated framework.
– These tokenized securities will retain the entitlements, investor protections
and ownership rights associated with their traditional form.
– NYSE has announced plans for a tokenized securities platform designed to
support 24/7 trading of US-listed equities and ETFs, with near-instant
settlement and stablecoin funding.
Benefits: Reduce barriers for foreign investors; automated post-trade processes
and longer trading hours; reduce information asymmetry (e.g., embedding listing

Risks & limitations
– Tokenized equities require clear legal ownership, enforceable investor rights
and alignment with existing securities rules.
– Under some third-party wrapper structures, the token provides exposure to a
share without making its holder a shareholder in the underlying company.
– Investors may therefore face issuer or custodian risk and may not receive the
same voting, dividend or other rights as direct shareholders.

– Companies like Figure Technologies and Tradable use blockchains to support

– Tokenization remains concentrated among a small number of issuers and lacks a

– Legal fragmentation, credit quality and servicing arrangements remain

and mortgage markets are limited.
Challenges
– A BIS study found that liquidity improvements can depend on platforms offering
to buy tokens back. These arrangements can help investors exit but expose
platforms to liquidity and solvency risks.

BARRIERS TO ADOPTION: Weak secondary market liquidity and lack of regulations
continue to challenge adoption
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research.

10
Main barriers to adoption
– Secondary-market liquidity remains low. Issuance
growth has not consistently translated into active
trading or deep markets.
– Regulatory uncertainty: Fragmented or incomplete
frameworks slow institutional adoption and complicate
integration with traditional financial infrastructure.
– Legal uncertainty persists over whether token
ownership conveys ownership of the underlying
asset or a claim on it.
– In segments like money market funds, rapid

cross-border growth can increase regulatory
arbitrage, capital-flow volatility and AML/CFT
compliance challenges. Policymakers must
adapt frameworks as the technology evolves.
– Fragmented infrastructure: Public blockchains,
permissioned institutional ledgers and hybrid platforms
often lack interoperability, fragmenting markets.
Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a joint
framework to address this in March 2026.
– Limited integration with existing custody, settlement,

and record-keeping arrangements prevents institutions
from scaling tokenized products.
– Proprietary infrastructure and centralized
controls also raise questions about ownership
and governance.

### Page 11

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
OUTLOOK: Tokenized capital markets could become a core part of financial by the 2030s
Conservative forecasts for tokenized real-world
assets (ex stablecoins)
Source: Deutsche Bank Research.
– Tokenization will modernize financial infrastructure rather than eliminate intermediaries. We expect a gradual
transition onto digital rails, with banks retaining key roles across payments, FX, treasury, financing, collateral

and trading settlement.
Assumption, on the positive side reflects…
– So far, tokenization has achieved its greatest scale in stablecoins, with activity concentrated in crypto trading
and payments gaining ground.
– Short-term: Adoption will be driven by tokenized US Treasuries and money market funds, expanding as yield-
bearing collateral in repo and margin arrangements.
– Long-term:  The opportunity extends beyond assets to the infrastructure supporting issuance, settlement,

custody, collateral management and repo markets.
– We are seeing 3 growth catalysts: (i) infrastructure: major financial market infrastructures (DTCC, NYSE,
Nasdaq) are integrating tokenization into core workflows; (ii) liquidity: Growth of regulated on-chain money
(stablecoins and tokenized deposits) providing a trusted settlement foundation; (iii) and regulation: US 2025
GENIUS ACT continues to encourage global digital asset adoption, although the failure of the CLARITY act to

advance yesterday likely rules out progress in this congressional session until Jan 2027. Looking ahead, focus
will turn back to the SEC and CFTC for crypto rules.
…Risks to adoption
– Accelerated liquidity runs: Tokenization may create a new form of systemic liquidity risk that spreads faster than
traditional finance.
– Money market funds yield-chasing: Declining interest rates or rising competition may push MMF issuers into
risker assts, less-liquid assets to boost yield, undermining their safety.

– Crypto contagion: Tight integration with crypto markets via stablecoin reserves and collateral risks transmitting
digital asset volatility to conventional financial markets.
– Operational risks: Smart-contract vulnerabilities, cyberattacks, network outages could halt trading and prevent
access to the underlying assets.
– Financial Crime: Traditional institutions must scale up compliance and AML controls to manage illicit activities
on decentralized networks.

## Document 282: Global Expansion of Chinese Construction Machinery_ Lessons from Brazil (2026-09-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Expansion of Chinese Construction Machinery_ Lessons from Brazil (2026-09-14).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Global Expansion of Chinese 
Construction Machinery:  
Lessons from Brazil

1 
Authors 
Deyun Ou 
Economist

Yi Xiong, Ph.D. 
Chief Economist

September 14, 2026 
Executive Summary 
China’s exports have been strong this year, surging by 19% YoY in the first 
eight months. Among these exports, heavy-asset capital goods have played a 
particularly important role. Using construction machinery as a representative 
example, we illustrate how stronger product competitiveness, Chinese overseas 
projects, and Chinese manufacturers’ “going-global” strategies have enabled 
market share gains across a broad range of economies. 
Chinese construction machinery has expanded across the globe since 2020. 
Exports of related products quadrupled between 2020 and 2025 and have 
remained on a strong upward trend this year. More importantly, Chinese 
products have achieved significant market-share gains across a broad range of 
economies and accounted for 26% of global imports in 2025 compared to 10% in 
2019. In this report, we aim to explore the factors behind this. 
We use Chinese excavator exports to Brazil as a case study. Excavators are 
one of the most representative categories of construction machinery, while Brazil 
is South America’s largest excavator importer and ranks among the world’s top 
20. Chinese excavators have also made remarkable market-share gains in Brazil, 
with their share rising from 20% to 90% in six years. Brazil therefore offers a 
compelling case study on this topic. 
Through the case study and our analysis of aggregate data, we identify three 
drivers of the global expansion of Chinese construction machinery: 
— Product competitiveness: Chinese brands have steadily improved both 
price advantage and product quality. 
— Demand from Chinese overseas projects: Chinese contractors are 
undertaking more infrastructure projects and naturally exhibit stronger 
preferences for Chinese equipment. 
— “Going global” strategy by Chinese companies: Higher profitability has 
encouraged them to direct more resources towards overseas expansion.

### Page 2

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 2

Introduction 
China’s exports have been strong this year, surging by 19% YoY in the first eight 
months. In a note published for clients in March, we identified several drivers of 
this export growth, including AI-related demand, energy-cost advantages and the 
green transition. In particular, heavy-asset capital goods have been an important 
contributor, with construction machinery offering a representative example. 
Chinese construction machinery has expanded rapidly across the globe since 
2020. Using HS8429 products —including excavators, bulldozers, graders and 
related equipment —as a proxy, export values quadrupled between 2020 and 
2025 (Figure 1). More importantly, the expansion has been broad -based: 128 of 
the 140 economies reporting HS8429 imports recorded an increase in China’s 
market share over the period (Figure 2). Chinese products now account for more 
than half of HS8429 imports in over 30 economies. As a result, China’s share of 
global HS8429 imports has risen from 10% in 2019 to 26% in 2025. 
Looking at  aggregate trade data alone  does not fully reveal the multiple 
underlying drivers. At the country level, economic structures, investment cycles 
and infrastructure demand vary substantially, leading to very different import 
patterns and market-share dynamics. At the product level, HS8429 encompasses 
a diverse range of machinery categories with different technologies, end-markets 
and competitive landscapes.  
Therefore, we adopt a case -study approach and focus on Chinese excavator 
exports to Brazil. We believe this is particularly representative since:  
• Excavators are highly representative of the construction machinery 
industry, accounting for half of China’s HS8429 exports. 
• Brazil is the largest excavator importer in South America and ranks 
among the top twenty importers globally. The demand is underpinned by 
three factors. 1) As a major mining economy, Brazil requires substantial 
earthmoving equipment. 2) Brazil is one of the world's largest agricultural 
producers. The farming sector relies heavily on capital investment and 
machinery to raise productivity. 3) The government has renewed its focus 
on infrastructure development in recent years.  
• Chinese excavator manufacturers have achieved exceptional market -
share gains  in Brazil. Their  share increased from 20% to 90% over the 
past six years  (Figure 4), with gains observed across all excavator 
segments.

### Page 3

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 3

expanded rapidly since 2020

Expansion of Chinese Excavators in Brazil  
Chinese excavators have become increasingly visible across Brazil . Their 
presence is no longer confined to mines, railways, and other construction sites. 
They are increasingly involved in landmark projects such as Rio Olympic venues 
and World Cup stadiums, and can even be found in residential backyards and 
farms. Chinese brands reportedly occupied the top three positions in Brazil’s 
market in 2025, while six Chinese brands ranked among the top ten. Customs 
data paint a stronger picture: Chinese products now account for ~90% of Brazil’s 
excavator imports, both by value and volume. 
China’s expansion appears to have been supported mainly by Brazil’s incremental 
demand rather than the outright displacement of competing suppliers. During the 
market recovery from 2017 to 2021, excavator imports rose from all major 
sources. From 2022 to 2025, imports from China grew much faster, driving a 
rapid increase in China’s market share, while annual imports from other 
suppliers—including Euro pe, Japan and Korea —remained broadly stable at 
around USD 100mn in total  (Figure 5). This does not mean that other suppliers

### Page 4

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 4

were unaffected by Chinese competition: without China’s sharp market -share 
gains, they would likely have captured a larger share of Brazil’s surge in excavator 
demand. 
Interestingly, the expansion has been different across segments  (Figure 6). 
Medium-sized excavators began gaining market share steadily after 2017, while 
mini excavators only entered a similar trajectory after 2019. Large excavators 
followed a different path. Prior to 2021, Chinese brands were absent, yet they 
rapidly gained traction after the first entry. The distinct patterns suggest that the 
rise of Chinese excavators might have been driven by multiple demand - and 
supply-side forces.

Figure 5: This expansion appears to have come largely 
from incremental demand rather than outright 
displacement

Figure 6: Expansion has been different across product 
segments

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Brazil Custom

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Brazil Custom. Brazil’s 8-digit NCM customs 
classification distinguishes excavators by power categories, allowing us to group them 
into three segments.

Factor 1: Price Advantage Meets Quality Upgrading  
A widening price advantage has been a major driver behind the expansion of 
Chinese products in the medium-sized and mini excavator segments. Since these 
products are relatively less technologically demanding, pricing remains a major 
consideration for buyers.  We divide the sample into four periods and  compare 
China’s price advantage against market share gains (Figure 7). China’s advantage 
has steadily expanded, with products now more than 50% cheaper than peers. 
Meanwhile, the relationship between price advantage and market share is highly 
significant. For medium -sized excavators, a one percentage point increase in 
China’s price advantage is associated with a 0.4pt gain in market share. For mini 
excavators, the corresponding relationship is even higher at 0.9pt. 
Indeed, mini excavators provide the clearest example of price -driven expansion. 
These machines are used for small -scale construction projects or purchased by 
individual consumers, making demand particularly price -sensitive. Chinese 
products began to undercut competitors after 2017, and their price advantage 
widened significantly after 2020  (Figure 8). The gap has now reached USD 
17,000 per unit . We further decompose this price advantage  (Figure 9). 60% of 
the widening gap reflects price increases by overseas competitors amid higher 
inflation, while 40% reflects price cuts by Chinese manufacturers. RMB

### Page 5

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 5

depreciation accounted for 10% of the gap during 2023-25. However, following 
its appreciation in 2026, the cumulative contribution has turned negative. 
More noteworthy is the advance of Chinese brands into the high -end segment. 
Large excavators are far more technologically sophisticated. Before 2021, 
Chinese brands were virtually absent from Brazil’s large excavators market. They 
entered the market in 2021 and expanded rapidly thereafter (Figure 10). So far in 
2026, 43 of Brazil’s 47 imported large excavators have come from China. Chinese 
manufacturers are reportedly delivering 100-tonne-class excavators to Brazilian 
mining customers and are actively promoting equipment in the 500-tonne 
category. Chinese companies ’ competitiveness is increasingly extending from 
price advantage toward technology intensity.

Figure 7: A widening price advantage has driven 
expansion in the medium-sized and mini excavator

Figure 8: Mini excavators provide the clearest example 
of price-driven expansion

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Brazil Custom. The four observations correspond to 
the periods 2006-2010, 2011-2015, 2016-2020, and 2021-2025, respectively.

to overseas inflation, domestic price cuts and RMB 
depreciation

market in 2021 and rapidly expanded thereafter

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Brazil Custom

Factor 2: Demand from Chinese Overseas Projects  
Chinese firms are undertaking more construction projects in Brazil, generating 
additional demand. They naturally have a stronger preference for Chinese-made 
machinery. The value of construction projects completed by Chinese companies 
has risen consistently since 2021. Growth in newly signed contracts has been

### Page 6

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 6

even stronger, with their value reaching a record USD 4.6bn in 2024  (Figure 11). 
This trend may have contributed to the growth in excavator imports since 2024, 
especially for large excavators. 
The Salvador -Itaparica Bridge project provides a compelling example. Once 
completed, it will be the longest sea -crossing bridge in South America. The 
project was awarded to two Chinese SOEs in 2019, with an initial investment of 
USD 2.2bn, which was raised to USD 2.7bn in 2025. It is the largest infrastructure 
investment undertaken by Chinese companies in Brazil. Since construction began 
this year, ~800 tons of equipment from China have arrived at the port of Salvador 
since April, consistent with the surge in custom data (Figure 12). Most recently on 
July 1, the project company showcased the first batch of equipment deployed on 
site, all sourced from China.

Figure 11: Chinese firms are undertaking more 
construction projects in Brazil

Figure 12: Imports of Chinese machinery through the 
Port of Salvador have surged in recent months

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, China Mofcom

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Brazil Custom

Factor 3: Supply-Side Expansion Driven by Higher Profitability 
Chinese firms have strong incentives to expand overseas because foreign 
markets offer higher profitability. Some views have argued that low export prices 
primarily reflect dumping driven by weak domestic demand. If that were the case, 
corporate profits would be expected to remain flat or deteriorate as firms 
sacrificed margins to gain market share. Company -level evidence suggests the 
opposite. 
We examine five listed Chinese construction machinery manufacturers.  
Combined overseas revenue has risen consistently since 2020, doubling in 2022 
and doubling again by 2025, broadly mirroring the trajectory of China’s total 
construction machinery exports (Figure 13). More importantly, overseas 
profitability has improved substantially. Across the five companies, operating 
margins in overseas markets have increased by 5-15 percentage points, with the 
average rising from 20% during the previous decade to 30% today (Figure 14). In 
contrast, domestic margins have fallen by 5 percentage points.

### Page 7

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 7

Figure 13: Overseas revenue has risen consistently 
since 2021

Figure 14: Overseas profit margins have exceeded 
domestic margins

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Wind

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Wind

Beyond Excavators: Chinese Construction Machinery 
Expansion 
Through this case study, we identify three forces underpinning the expansion of 
Chinese excavators in Brazil: improving price and quality competitiveness, 
growing demand from Chinese overseas projects, and proactive going -global 
strategies by Chinese companies. 
We believe the forces extend to the broader construction machinery sector.   
• Price and Quality Competitiveness. The price advantage over HS8429 
has become more pronounced since 2022  (Figure 15). The analysis of 
eight-digit categories revealed a more comprehensive picture  (Figure 
16). First, exports increased across all categories, including those with 
rising prices. Second, the products recording larger price increases 
tended to have higher unit values. That said, the expansion is driven by 
price cuts as well as increasing demand for higher-value products.  
• Overseas Expansion of Chinese Enterprises . Chinese overseas 
construction project s have reaccelerated since 2024, with completed 
value and newly signed contracts rising by 20% and 15%, respectively  
(Figure 17). Belt and Road economies are key destinations for Chinese 
overseas construction projects, and Chinese construction machinery has 
gained market share much more rapidly in these markets. 
• Proactive “Going global” strategy . Since the 2010s, construction 
machinery manufacturers have viewed overseas expansion as a key pillar 
of future growth and have invested heavily in international distribution 
channels, service networks and localized production facilities. 
Looking ahead, we believe all three drivers are likely to remain in place for an 
extended period. China’s manufacturing capability and continued technological 
progress should support further gains in product competitiveness. Accelerating 
component localization could further strengthen the price advantage. Meanwhile, 
the overseas expansion of Chinese companies is expected to remain a long-term 
trend. Belt and Road economies warrant particular attention, as Chinese

### Page 8

14 September 2026 
China Macro

Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 8

investment in these markets has surged in recent years, laying a solid foundation 
for further overseas expansion (Figure 18). As a result, we remain constructive on 
the outlook for construction machinery exports.  
That said, several risks warrant monitoring. First, further RMB appreciation could 
erode the price competitiveness of Chinese products. Second, the sharp increase 
in market share achieved in Brazil —from 20% to 90% —may not be easily 
replicated elsewhere. In some markets, Chinese firms could face stron ger 
competition from local manufacturers, limiting the pace of further market -share 
gains.

Figure 15: China's price advantage on HS8429 has 
become more pronounced since 2022

Figure 16: The expansion is driven by price cuts and 
increasing demand for higher-value products

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, UN Comtrade

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, CEIC. Each bubble represents an eight-digit category 
under HS8429.

Figure 17: Chinese contractors have been undertaking 
more projects overseas since 2024

## Document 307: Meta的灵感Muse，只比“AI点咖啡”高明一点点

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta的灵感Muse，只比“AI点咖啡”高明一点点.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Meta 推出的个⼈ Agent“Muse” 因主打省钱与易⽤登顶榜⾸，其本质仍是通过云端虚拟机与
电脑操作技术执⾏任务，形态与国内早期的 “AI 点咖啡 ” 及 AutoGLM 等产品类似。虽然 Muse
在场景选择和产品体验上更具巧思，但仍⾯临隐私信任、平台封锁（如亚⻢逊拦截）及商
业化变现等⾏业底层难题。
摘要：
Muse 成了巨头 Meta 的 “ 灵感⼥神 ” ，靠⼀⼰之⼒，把巨头 Meta 的股价拉升了 15% 。
Meta 旗下智能体 Muse 9 ⽉ 8 ⽇发布，⾄ 9 ⽉ 18 ⽇，登上美国苹果 App Store 免费榜第⼀，连续三
天占据榜⾸。到 9 ⽉ 21 ⽇，把 Meta 股价推向⾼潮，单⽇上涨 11.4% ，增加约 1923 亿美元市值。
它的热度甚⾄传导到了半导体⾏业，连 CPU 也成了市场追逐的对象，带动 AMD 上涨 9.9% ，市值
⾸次越过 1 万亿美元。
对中国⽤⼾来说，这⼀幕却有些似曾相识。从 “ ⼀句话点外卖 ” 到跨应⽤⽐价、下单，过去⼀年，

个⼈ Agent （能⾃主执⾏任务的 AI 智能体）已经演⽰过不少令⼈兴奋的场景。甚⾄号称天⽣就是
AI 个⼈助⼿的个⼈终端 “ ⾖包⼿机 ” 也出现了，尝试让 AI 直接操作屏幕，替⽤⼾在不同 App 之间完
成任务。
很多⼈都喝过发布会上 “AI 请客 ” 的咖啡。
但现实的体验还是⾚裸裸地残酷。发布会演⽰中流畅的连续操作，到了⽇常使⽤中，可能卡在
登录、⽀付或⼀次突然出现的弹窗上；银⾏等应⽤的限制让 AI 助⼿也完全绕不过去。
如今 Muse 带着相似的产品承诺登场，甚⾄很快遭到 Amazon 封锁。为什么这些熟悉的能⼒和障
碍，到了美国市场，⼜被赋予了如此⼤的想象空间？
图： Meta ⾸席⼈⼯智能官  Alexandr Wang （汪滔）和 Muse ，图⽚由 AI ⽣成
让⼈下载的理由，是 “ 帮我找回钱 ”
Muse 于 9 ⽉ 8 ⽇发布，随后⼗天登顶。传播中反复出现的主⻆，是退款、闲置订阅和保险账单。
Meta ⾸席 AI 官 Alexandr Wang 推动 “MuseMoneyChallenge” （ Muse 省钱挑战），⿎励分享省钱结

果。外媒报道，⼀名创业者称， Muse 帮他找到⼀年便宜 3500 美元的⻋险⽅案；但是，同时，报
道也提醒，这⾥⾯的许多分享其实来⾃ Meta 员⼯。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

但是这类案例给了⽤⼾可以做尝试的理由：检查有没有⽩交的钱。对于不关⼼ AI 的⼈，节省⾦额
⽐⼀串模型的 benchmark 成绩更容易理解，也更适合转发。
Muse 的产品传播还在解决另⼀个障碍：⽤⼾不知道该交给 AI 什么任务。 Muse 设计团队披露，早
期测试中，功能太多反⽽让⼈⽆从下⼿，于是他们加⼊了根据⽬标和对话主动⽣成建议的设
计。科技记者 Alex Heath 也在公开帖⽂中肯定 Ideas 和 Feed ⻚⾯，因为它们会提出⾃⼰想不到的
任务。
沃顿商学院教授 Ethan Mollick 的评价是：其他 AI 实验室的系统已经能完成类似事情， Muse 的专
注设计让普通⼈更容易上⼿；Meta 同时投⼊了⼤量免费算⼒⽀持体验。
C 端的玩法，显然 Meta 更擅⻓，给⽤⼾提供直⽩的吸引⼒ “ 主打省钱 ” ，功能设计上让⽤⼾简单上
⼿，⼊⼝是最熟悉的 whatsapp ，更重要的是还有不花钱的 token 可以挥霍。⽤⼾可以先免费试⽤
Muse ，也能像给联系⼈发消息⼀样，在 WhatsApp ⾥让它处理任务，降低了尝试和使⽤的⻔

槛。
省钱案例、任务建议和熟悉的⼊⼝，构成了这轮传播的⼏个值得关注的环节。⽽且，省钱故事
提供了传播导⽕索，传导到榜单的表现上⼜成为投资者重新评估 Meta 的信号。
技术的新意，究竟落在哪⾥？
那么， Muse 究竟有没有技术上的新意呢？
模型负责理解要求、安排步骤，再调⽤浏览器等⼯具打开⽹⻚、填写表单。这台电脑运⾏在
Meta 的服务器上，因此⽤⼾关掉⼿机应⽤，任务仍可继续。 Meta 通过虚拟机技术，为每位⽤⼾
划出独⽴的运⾏空间，保存个⼈数据和任务进度。 Computer Use （电脑操作能⼒）可以让 AI 能
够使⽤这台电脑上的软件。
Muse 的 “ 云端电脑 ” 已经有先例。根据 OpenAI 官⽅⽂档明确， ChatGPT Work 运⾏在基于虚拟机
的沙盒中，执⾏状态关联⽤⼾，环境可跨任务复⽤，也可在保留部分状态后更换。其云端浏览
器在⽤⼾关闭电脑后仍能继续⼯作。 GPT-6 还可以编写代码操作浏览器与桌⾯，配合插件完成任
务。
但是 Muse 的⼯程特点，是围绕个⼈数据划定权限边界。按 Meta 披露，每位⽤⼾拥有 Muse

Secure VM （ Muse 安全虚拟机），作为个⼈数据的主要存储位置。执⾏程序和⼯具被放进虚拟
机内部的受限容器，账⼾凭据、安全检查及持久状态分别管理；模型推理通过接⼝调⽤外部基
础设施，并⾮整个⼤模型都装在个⼈虚拟机⾥。
我们可以把它理解为⼀个分区的⼯作间， Agent 能在操作区读⽂件、运⾏程序，账⼾凭据由另⼀
处保管，连接器动作和⽹络访问还要经过 Sentinel （权限审核智能体）。这样设计，是为了让处
理陌⽣⽹⻚的程序即使受到误导，也难以直接拿到全部权限。但是，实际安全效果仍需测试验
证。
这样设计的⼯程价值，在于将执⾏能⼒与授权边界⼀同组织起来：服务已有接⼝时调⽤连接
器，没有合适接⼝时尝试浏览器操作。接⼝需要合作接⼊，界⾯操作则要应对⻚⾯变化与验
证。
这套设计服务于持续委托，保留进度，按时间或相关事件继续处理事务，在关键动作前请求授
权。独⽴虚拟机、容器和权限审核都有既有技术基础；⽬前⻚还⽆法证明 Muse 的整套⽅案领先
所有竞争者。

### Page 3

图： Muse 安全架构⽰意图。每位⽤⼾拥有独⽴的云端虚拟机；任务执⾏程序与账⼾凭据、持久
数据分开管理， Sentinel 负责审核对外访问及连接器操作。
国内很早就做过类似形态的产品尝试， 2025 年的 AutoGLM 2.0 同时提供云⼿机、云电脑，覆盖
移动应⽤、⽹⻚与办公任务；⾖包⼿机直接买点就是 “AI 原⽣⼿机 ” ，可以操作⽤⼾⼿机⾥的
app ；千问 app 利⽤阿⾥本⾝的⽣态优势，将点单、⽀付等服务通过合作接⼝接⼊，减少逐屏点
击。
界⾯操作与接⼝调⽤可以结合，任务也能在本机或云端执⾏。 AutoGLM 的 “ 云电脑 ” 指供 AI 操作的
环境， Muse 的 “ 独⽴ Linux 虚拟机 ” 主要描述它怎样为⽤⼾划分运⾏空间，两者处在不同的⽐较层
⾯。
Muse 说明，部分 AI ⼿机体验可以通过现有⼿机和云端服务交付；要调⽤⼿机⾥的应⽤、相机和
系统权限，仍需设备端⽀持与⽤⼾授权。
看起来， Muse 本质还是那个国内⽤⼾熟知的，擅⻓表演点咖啡的 AI 助⼿。只是，这个 AI 助⼿在

国内，暂时没有办法点燃⽤⼾和资本的兴奋点了。
换⼀种思路，不⽌点咖啡
Muse ⾯向个⼈，当前主推⽣活管理，但它也能⽣成⽂档和⽹⻚。 “ 个⼈⽤⼾ ” 和 “ ⽣活任务 ” 并⾮同
⼀个概念，⼀个⼈的助⼿完全可以同时处理家庭与⼯作。
中国也没有放弃⽣活 Agent 。阿⾥披露，千问春节期间促成近 2 亿笔订单；活动伴随 30 亿元激
励，订单量不能单独证明⾃然需求与盈利。与此同时，⾖包在 6 ⽉推出⾯向复杂办公的专业版，
连续包⽉ 68 元起，腾讯推出 WorkBuddy 企业版。国内在并⾏探索⽣活与办公场景，只是办公场
景的⻛⼝更⼤。

### Page 4

办公更容易 “ 收费 ” ，整理⼀批表格、完成调研，节省的劳动可以与省掉的交付时间做⽐较。但是
点⼀杯奶茶原本只需⼏次点击， AI 带来的增量便利有限，出错后接⼿反⽽增加⿇烦。对服务平
台⽽⾔，这类任务仍可能通过订单转化产⽣价值。
Muse在场景的选择上更聪明，⽐如取消忘记的订阅、⽐较续保报价、追踪退款、协调家庭⽇
程。这些事频率未必⾼，但是能省钱、省时间和省⼼，潜⼒在于替⽤⼾处理⻓期拖延的事务，
避免漏掉截⽌时间。
Muse 设计者举过⼀个亲⾝案例：助⼿整理孩⼦开学邮件与学校⽹站时，发现⼀项体育选拔报名
即将截⽌，及时提醒了她。
反观国内的⽣活服务已有不少便捷流程，这可能压低常规点单的增量价值。但保险续费、 App 订
阅、售后申诉、照护协调这些价值，仍然值得去验证。
图： Muse 运⾏的官⽅演⽰视频
这⻔⽣意真的成⽴了吗？
Stratechery 创始⼈ Ben Thompson 在 9 ⽉ 21 ⽇表⽰， Muse 是他试过最容易上⼿的个⼈ Agent 。它
使⽤的 Muse Spark 1.3 尚未达到最前沿模型⽔平，却已能⽀撑很好的产品体验。在他看来，⽤

⼾⼀旦把账⼾信息交给助⼿，并让它参与⽇常事务，更换产品的成本就会提⾼。
Meta 同时也研发基础模型。这轮热度更直接地反映了市场对模型 + 应⽤消费应⽤前景的重新估
值，当模型能⼒达到可⽤⽔平后， Meta 能否凭借产品体验和分发渠道，让⽤⼾持续委托任务，
并从中获得收⼊。 Truist 分析师预测， Muse 到 2030 年可为 Meta 带来 285 亿美元增量收⼊。
Oppenheimer 分析师 Jason Helfstein 维持中性判断：付费⼈群可能已有 ChatGPT 或 Gemini 订
阅，信任也是⻔槛，需要约 1.15 亿付费⽤⼾，才能显著影响 Meta 盈利；模型采⽤每⽉ 20 美元和
80% 增量营业利润率假设。免费算⼒越多，利润假设就越难达到。
但这也从侧⾯解释了⾏情为何能外溢：市场在重估消费 Agent 的规模。 CPU 和内存同样受这种预
期影响。模型推理之外，浏览器、代码和调度需要 CPU（中央处理器）；维持运⾏中的程序需要
DRAM （服务器运⾏内存）。并发任务越多，两类负载可能越⼤。

⽤⼾没有任务时， Meta 可以暂停云端虚拟机，把任务进度保存到硬盘；收到新任务后再恢复。
因此， “ 每⼈⼀台虚拟机 ” 不意味着每⼈全天占⽤固定的 CPU 和内存。 Muse 的热度让投资者预期
更多⼈会使⽤这类服务，但实际需要采购多少芯⽚，还取决于有多少任务同时运⾏、每项任务
要运⾏多久。

### Page 5

Muse 还是没有解决根本难题
Muse 把⽤⼾的数据和执⾏环境放进专属云端虚拟机，但是⽤⼾敢不敢真的让它⻓期接触邮件、
订单和⽀付信息？据外媒报道，内部测试中的 Muse 曾在监测票务约 15 分钟后停⽌刷新，也出现
过私⼈数据暴露问题。 Meta 确认，曾为提⾼安全性推迟了原定 4 ⽉的发布。
即使⽤⼾愿意授权， Agent 也未必能进⼊所有服务。 9 ⽉ 21 ⽇， Amazon 阻⽌ Muse 代⽤⼾浏览和

可，平台依然有能⼒决定 Agent 能否进⼊⾃⼰的服务。

United States: Activity shrugs off the energy shock
03/09/2026 4
July 2026: Business sentiment holds up in
services
August 2026: Manufacturing activity firmly
anchored in expansion territory
July 2026: Persistent pressure on input prices August 2026: Supplier delivery times lengthen

again
July 2026: Concerns over household finances
ease again
July 2026: Inflation excluding energy back at its
pre-war level
The US economy has held up well since the shock began. Consumption and business investment grew at a 4.1% annualized
pace in Q2. At the same time, the scope for energy-driven disinflation has narrowed: WTI (the US reference) has averaged
USD 82/bbl since 8 July, ranging between USD 72-92.
CPI rose 0.1% m/m in July, reversing June’s -0.4% drop. The year-on-year rate eased to 3.4% (down 0.1pp) but remained

1.0pp higher than in February – the last pre-war reading. Energy accounts for the entire gap: its contribution to the annual
rate was negligible in February and stood at 1.0pp in July. Inflation excluding energy reached 2.55% y/y in July (down
0.1pp), unchanged from February. Macroeconomic conditions remain far less inflationary than in 2022, although small
businesses have raised their pricing intentions.
Business sentiment strengthened despite the shock. The ISM manufacturing index has remained in growth territory

without interruption since January 2026, following 36 months of contraction out of 38. Input prices – a leading indicator of
inflation – and delivery times have tightened further, as the Middle East situation compounds existing pressures from steel
and aluminum prices, tariffs, and input availability.
Household confidence remains deeply depressed – a long-standing trend – and sensitivity to gasoline prices rules out any
improvement in the near-term. Real consumer spending, however, has never contracted monthly since the onset of the

conflict.
Source: BLS, ISM, NFIB, University of Michigan, BNP Paribas
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022.
Unit: inflation change in percentage point, point for confidence surveys.

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
The most significant development this month has been the
broad rise in global bond yields. This is occurring amid a
longer-run uptrend in real yields, inflation expectations and
term premia since the low-rate pre-pandemic era. The latest

move comes on the back of renewed conflict in the Middle
East, oil prices pushing back above US$100/bbl and central
banks actively contemplating more rate rises. With mid-term
elections adding another layer of political disruption in the
US and bond markets defying direct attempts by the US
administration to lower yields, it’s safe to say the situation is
‘heating up’.
Inflation remains materially above target across all of the G7
bar Japan, US 10-year yields are closing in on 5%, and fiscal

conditions remain unsettling, most notably in the US where
gross debt levels recently surpassed US$40 trillion while
federal deficits continue to run at 6% of GDP.
These developments made for a provocative backdrop at Fed
Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole debut, where he emphasised
that inflation remains the Committee’s priority. Markets are
now pricing three to four hikes into 2027, though we believe
only two will be needed to lean against any deterioration in

expectations and the run higher in long-term yields. Europe
faces similar concerns, but in our view, restrictive credit
conditions argue against an aggressive follow-through – one
more hike in Q4 should suffice. China’s challenge is near the
complete opposite: policy is already highly accommodative
and households are not short of cash, but confidence remains
too fragile for precautionary saving to give way to stronger
consumption and investment. Concerted action by authorities

is required if they want to minimise the risk of a prolonged low-
growth scenario.
Closer to home, the RBA narrative has also shifted. A stronger-
than-expected July CPI, a relatively resilient consumer and a
larger data-centre investment pipeline has prompted us to
reinstate a November rate hike into our base case. September
remains a close call, but we expect the Board will prefer to wait
for the quarterly CPI before moving.
Australia: The Q2 National Accounts showed Australia’s

economy expanded 0.4% in Q2 and 2.1% in year-ended terms.
It was the first full quarter that households and businesses had
to deal with the global energy shock flowing from the Middle
East conflict alongside and higher domestic interest rates. With
a strong ramp up in data centre investment also coming off a
massive surge in Q1, the result was a messy and noisy quarter.
Looking through this, growth appears to be stabilising at a
slow pace rather than stalling.

Commodities: Most commodities rose in August as the
geopolitical risk premium reasserted itself, lifting our broadest
commodities index 4.5%mth, with steelmaking commodities
the notable exception. Reflecting ongoing disruption to global
oil markets, we have revised our Brent forecast higher, with
prices now expected to average US$90/bbl over the remainder
of 2026.
Global FX Markets: Market participants remain acutely
aware of risk. At the same time, confidence in US economic

growth remains resolute. Nonetheless, the US dollar has
edged lower over the past month.
New Zealand: The economy appears to be shaking off the
disruption caused by the Middle East conflict and continuing
the recovery that began last year. At the same time, the
conflict has driven headline inflation well above the top of
the RBNZ’s target range, leading it to begin removing policy
stimulus six months earlier than it had previously projected.
We expect the OCR to reach 3% by year-end and move higher

in 2027.
United States: A lack of confidence in the guidance given
by Chair Warsh and open-ended Middle East conflict has led
participants to almost price four rate hikes. We instead see
two as necessary to calm concerns and give disinflation time.
China: Assessed in isolation, the domestic demand trend
could be interpreted as suggesting policy is restrictive. This is
not the case. Rather, confidence is holding China back.
Asia: Economic fundamentals across Asia have strengthened,

yet currencies have struggled to keep pace. The explanation
lies less in growth and more in capital allocation. Policymakers
are shifting their focus from attracting to retaining capital.
Europe & UK: Current data and financial conditions argue for
only one more hike then a steady hand from the ECB in contrast
to the almost four hikes the market has recently priced.
Heating up

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The key development in global markets this month has been
the sharp rise in bond yields. Major economy (ex. Japan)
10yr yields have lifted 30–40bps since the previous Market
Outlook, with most of the move occurring in recent days. UK

and Australian sovereign 10yr yields have both risen above
5% and US 10yr Treasuries are almost at that mark.
The background to the latest increase is that yields have
been trending up for most of the post-pandemic period,
especially outside the US, as economies exited the previous
low-inflation, negative-yield regimes. Both real yields and
implied inflation expectations increased during this longer-run
transition, as did estimated term premia.
The increase in recent days is partly a reflection of shifting

views about the near-term monetary policy outlook. The
shorter ends of yield curves have typically lifted more than
long ends, narrowing 2–10yr spreads. Longer-term market
inflation expectations implied by both bond and swap markets
have increased in recent days but remain well below the
levels reached in the early months of the US–Iran conflict. The
increase in real long yields in recent days has been particularly
marked for Australia, where markets have repriced the chance

of a near-term rate hike following higher-than-expected
inflation in July (see below). This week’s ECB hike and hawkish
rhetoric has also influenced euro area pricing.
Several underlying drivers have been at play in the
recalibration of views about central bank action. Markets
have been unnerved by the unusual manoeuvrings of the
US Treasury in support of the US bond market, which have
drawn attention to the US fiscal incontinence that was already

evident a few years ago and has worsened under the current
administration. More broadly, the US government is making
an exceptional call on global savings, and higher defence
spending by other countries is adding to this. We expect more
manoeuvres by the US authorities in an effort to contain bond
yields and so the government’s debt-servicing burden, but
these will not be a lasting solution.
An additional factor that has grown over the past couple of

years and come to a head recently is the demand for debt
finance coming from the ‘hyperscaler’ tech firms – Amazon,
Google, Microsoft, Meta and other majors at the epicentre of the
AI boom. By way of example, Alphabet (Google’s parent) issued
the largest-ever deal into the $A corporate bond market in
recent weeks, and others are likely to follow. These deals have
attracted strong demand but have done so at very wide spreads
to sovereign yields given the issuer ratings. While yields on the

bonds of other corporates have so far not repriced, the demand
for finance represents a digestion issue for global investors,
with implications for sovereign yields as well.
Global bond yields rise ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... while RBA outlook pivots back to hikes
Both the atmospherics and the substance around the RBA
policy decision have shifted considerably since the August
Market Outlook  was published. The RBA’s August inflation
forecasts implied a base case of rates remaining on hold.

However, subsequent communication made clear that the
Monetary Policy Board (MPB) would raise rates again if upside
risks to inflation emerged, and we advised that a November
rate hike should not be ruled out entirely.
Since then, wages data undershot RBA expectations and
consumer confidence and business conditions have relapsed to
weakness. Markets have instead focused on the stronger-than-
expected July CPI data and signs that the consumer has been

more resilient than expected. A rate hike in November is now
fully priced in, with a significant chance it occurs in September,
as well as some probability of a follow-up hike in the new year.
We have also revised our RBA view, bringing a November hike
back into the base case, rather than being a risk, as previously
expected. The main reasons for the shift are the growing
evidence of a more resilient household sector, and a larger-
than-expected impetus from the data centre boom. National

accounts and internal data have pointed to stronger household
incomes in Q2 and onwards, which means spending will be
more resilient over the near term even with sentiment stuck at
historically weak levels. While the housing market is looking
weaker than our previously published forecasts, its impact on
consumer spending is more than offset by the wider boost to
growth coming from a globally-driven surge in tech-related
spending. An unprecedented pipeline of investment in data

centres and associated renewable electricity generation and
distribution is expected to drive business investment and so
GDP growth – but also limit the pace of disinflation.
Tactically, we believe the RBA leadership would strongly
favour a November hike over September. Overreacting to a
noisy monthly print is something they have previously said
they would not do. That said, if the internal members felt the
situation was more urgent and wanted to get the hike done

in September, we believe they could muster a majority of
Monetary MPB votes in favour. Either way, the September
decision may see a split vote, with some members coming
into the meeting with different views about supply capacity
and the state of the labour market, and more concerned about
developments in global bond markets. The probability of a
September hike is not zero, but we do not think it is the most
likely outcome. We expect the MPB to prefer to wait for the

full quarterly inflation data and revised forecasts to confirm
the need for a rate increase.
We have not changed our assessment of the timing or number
of cuts to the cash rate, pencilling in three 25bp cuts starting
August 2027. This leaves the whole path for the cash rate
25bps higher than our earlier expectations, consistent with the
emerging impetus from business investment.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
The June quarter National Accounts showed Australia’s
economy expanded 0.4% in Q2 and 2.1% in year-ended terms.
It was the first full quarter that households and businesses
had to deal with the global energy shock flowing from the

conflict in the Middle East, and higher domestic interest rates
following the February, March and May rate hikes. With a
strong ramp up in data centre investment also coming off
a massive surge in Q1, the result was a messy and noisy
quarter. Nevertheless, looking through this, the Accounts
point to growth stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling.
The household sector has been resilient with solid income
growth providing support. Meanwhile the private investment

upswing clearly has further to run.
Given the firmer starting position, we have revised higher our
near-term forecasts for economic growth and inflation, with
labour market conditions also expected to remain slightly
firmer through 2026 (see here). For 2027, the impacts of
the sharper-than-expected downturn in established house
prices is expected to only partly offset strength elsewhere.
Importantly, we now expect the RBA Board to hike in
November 2026, with the cash rate peaking at 4.6% before

policy is cautiously eased in the second half of 2027.
Business takes the growth baton
The business investment upswing continues to gather
momentum and now appears to be generating larger positive
spillovers to related industries including construction,
professional services, wholesale trade, logistics and
manufacturing. The core drivers continue to be a major ramp
up in investment in data centres and renewable energy. New
business investment is growing at 10.5%yr, the fastest pace

since the December quarter of 2012 (excluding the pandemic).
Consistent with the upward revision to surveyed capex
intentions, we now expect new business investment to grow
by almost 13% in 2026 (up from 11.7%yr previously) and a
further 10% in 2027 (up from 7.5%yr). The cumulative rise of
almost 25% is set to be the sharpest two-year gain since the
March quarter of 2013.
And the near-term risks remain skewed to the upside. The
catalyst would be a second wave of renewable energy

investment, sparked by the rapid expansion of data centres
and the emergence of a large, ongoing source of electricity
demand. In this scenario, year-ended growth in business
investment could be stronger in 2027 than in 2026, providing a
further boost to GDP growth and labour market conditions.
Growth holds up despite headwinds ...
Matthew Hassan, Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
Pat Bustamante, Senior Economist
This investment surge, which is largely unrelated to the

domestic economic cycle and interest rates, is large enough for
the business sector to become the dominant driver of growth.
Of course, a significant portion of this investment will be met by
imports rather than domestic production. Both data centres and
renewables investment are highly import-intensive. However,
even with these ‘leakages’ the investment upturn still generates
substantial domestic activity, much like the LNG investment
boom did in the late 2010s (see Box, page 9).

Our modelling (see here) suggests that around half of total
investment in data centres translates into domestic value
added, with much of that occurring in the early stages of
the investment cycle when the structures housing high-
tech equipment are being built. Notably, the industries that
stand to benefit the most, including professional services,

### Page 7

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
... business takes the growth baton ...
manufacturing, construction and wholesale trade, are
already showing a turnaround in employment, wages and
profitability (see chart below). This suggests that some of
these interlinkages have emerged more quickly, and on a larger

scale, than previously thought, and are likely contributing to
the stronger-than-expected growth in labour income.
With growth in profit subdued – total (including mining)
profits have basically tracked sideways over the last 3½
years – how are businesses funding this investment?
Following a period of balance sheet strengthening associated
with pandemic support, businesses are increasingly drawing
on debt to expand operations and fund capital expenditure,

with leverage ratios moving back towards more normal levels.
This is evident in the continued strength of business credit
growth, which has sustained even as higher interest rates have
weighed on more interest-sensitive parts of the economy.
For the global hyperscalers at the epicentre of the boom – the
likes of Amazon, Google, Meta and Oracle – revenue growth
has been strong rather than weak. This, and surging equity
valuations, have improved access to capital markets. Australia

has benefited from this trend, with major players such as
Alphabet (Google’s parent company) issuing in the local
market, with further issuance likely as the data centre build-
out continues.
Construction activity holds firm despite softer
house prices
Australia’s construction sector is an important bellwether for
how various investment cycles are netting out domestically.
Both the current pulse and outlook remain positive. Aside from
the data centre and renewable energy build-out, non-residential

building is being supported by the need to cater for a growing
population, while residential building is also being supported
by a range of state and Federal government initiatives. This is
more than offsetting the predicted gradual wind-down in public
infrastructure spending.
Dwelling construction surprised on the upside in Q2, with the
pipeline continuing to expand despite headwinds from higher
interest rates, cost increases associated with the conflict in

the Middle East, rising labour costs and falling established
house prices. Although those headwinds remain an issue, we
continue to expect housing construction to remain relatively
resilient near term, supported by a range of state and federal
government policy initiatives, including changes to zoning
regulations and fast-track approvals.
Note that we have revised our house price forecasts lower – to
reflect both recent weakness and our updated interest rate

profile. We now expect house prices to fall by around 6% in
2026, resulting in a peak-to-trough decline of just over 7%.
The adjustment is expected to be somewhat larger in Sydney
and Melbourne, where prices are projected to decline by closer
to 10%. We continue to expect lower on-market supply and a
persistently tight housing market to limit further price declines.
As the RBA begins easing policy in the second half of 2027,
housing demand is expected to recover, supporting a gradual

lift in house prices through 2028 (see Table 1, page 8).
Despite this, we have left our dwelling construction forecasts
unchanged. As we noted in previous reports, the downturn in
established markets does have broader implications beyond
new dwelling construction. In particular, the spillover effects
on wealth are set to be more of a dampening effect on
consumer demand, particularly we move into 2027–28.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
Putting it all together: from headwinds to tailwinds
Aggregate economic conditions have firmed despite significant
headwinds. Business-led activity, which appears insensitive to
interest rates, is providing the economy with a tailwind strong

enough to support broader areas of the economy that are not
directly tied to the data centre/renewables build-out.
As a result, we have upgraded our near-term outlook to
1.5%yr in 2026, up from the 1.0%yr pace we anticipated prior
to the Q2 National Accounts. The outlook beyond 2026 is
largely unchanged, although the risks are tilted to the upside
given the size of the potential increase in renewable energy
investment. We expect growth to accelerate to 1.7%yr in 2027

and 2.5%yr in 2028 as the RBA eases rates in the second half
of 2027 in response to emerging spare capacity.
Reflecting the firmer near-term outlook, we have revised our
forecast peak in the unemployment rate down from 4.9% (in
Q1 2027) to 4.8% (in Q2 2027), where it is expected to hold
around this level throughout 2027. The slightly tighter labour
market, combined with stronger global inflationary pressures
associated with the AI investment boom, points to a firm

near-term inflation pulse of 3.3%yr in 2026, with quarterly
trimmed mean inflation expected to come in at 0.9%qtr in Q3
2026. Trimmed mean inflation is expected to ease to 2.7%yr
in 2027 and 2.3%yr in 2028 as spare capacity emerges and
productivity gradually improves.
Firmer economic and labour market conditions are supporting
household incomes and spending. We expect household
spending to grow by 2.0%yr in 2026, up from 1.7%yr
previously. The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker  suggests

spending momentum strengthened through Q2 and into Q3. A
rebound in tourism-related spending and income boosts from
award-related wage increases and the Federal Government’s
tax cuts should see household consumption rise by a
significant 0.8%qtr in Q3.
We expect consumption growth to remain at 2.0%yr in 2027,
supported by lower interest rates in the second half of the
year. We expect this momentum to carry into 2028, with
consumption growth accelerating to 2.9% in 2028.

By 2028, we expect to see signs of AI-driven productivity
gains emerge. Initially this is likely to be in where tasks are
augmented or automated by AI adoption. The fuller impacts
will occur when AI adoption allows for the creation of entirely
new goods and services, but this will take time. In addition, we
expect productivity growth to gradually improve as non-market
sector expansion peaks, the benefits of recent public and
private investment begin to materialise, and the labour market

gradually eases.
